TITLE: Marbella Boat Charter Weather in December: What to Expect
DESCRIPTION: Planning a marbella boat charter weather december? Expect 15-18°C, calm seas, and blue skies. Book your winter cruise today.

Home › Guide › Marbella Boat Charter Weather in December: What to Expect By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers Planning a marbella boat charter weather december ? You might be surprised how pleasant a winter cruise can be. With average highs of 18°C, six hours of daily sunshine, and calm Mediterranean seas, December offers a unique opportunity to enjoy the Costa del Sol without the summer crowds. All our boats depart year-round from Puerto Banús, and this guide covers exactly what to expect—temperatures, wind, sea conditions, and how to dress for a comfortable day on the water. December Temperatures in Marbella Daytime temperatures in Marbella during December typically range from 15°C to 18°C. Early mornings and late afternoons can dip to around 10°C, so the sweet spot for chartering is between 11am and 4pm. The sea temperature hovers at 16-17°C—swimmable for the brave, but most guests prefer to stay dry and enjoy the heated cabin or wrap up on deck. The sun feels warm when it's out, especially in sheltered spots, and the light is perfect for photography, with La Concha mountain often crystal clear in the background. Wind and Sea Conditions December is generally a calm month for the Mediterranean along the Marbella coast. Prevailing winds are light, usually 10-15 knots, and seas are flat to slight. The occasional Levante wind can pick up, but your licensed skipper will adjust the route to keep the ride comfortable. The coastline from Puerto Banús to Cabopino and Estepona is well-sheltered. For larger groups, the Boat Rental Marbella fleet includes stable vessels like the Azimut 58 Flybridge (17.6m, 12 pax) and the Mangusta 80 (24m, 12 pax), which handle any chop with ease. Sunshine and Daylight Hours December offers around 6 hours of direct sunshine per day, with sunrise at approximately 8:20am and sunset by 6:10pm. This gives you a solid window for a half-day or full-day charter. Cloud cover is variable, but many December days are partly cloudy with long sunny spells. Rain is infrequent—only 4-6 rainy days on average—so the odds are in your favour. A 4-hour charter (e.g., 11am-3pm) is a popular choice, allowing you to enjoy the best light and warmth. What to Wear and Bring Layering is the golden rule for a December boat charter. Start with a long-sleeve top or light jumper, add a windproof jacket or fleece, and wear comfortable trousers or jeans. Non-slip shoes are recommended. Don't forget sunglasses and sunscreen—the UV index can still be moderate, and glare off the water is strong. A light scarf or hat can help against the sea breeze. TITLE: Cabopino Dolphin Watching: Morning vs Afternoon Boat Tours in Marbella
DESCRIPTION: Cabopino dolphin watching morning vs afternoon Marbella: compare light, sea conditions, and wildlife activity. Book your 2h or 8h charter from Puerto Banús.

Home › Guide › Cabopino Dolphin Watching: Morning vs Afternoon Boat Tours in Marbella By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers Cabopino dolphin watching morning vs afternoon Marbella – which time slot delivers the best experience? The answer depends on your priorities: calm seas and golden light in the morning, or warmer water and active dolphins in the afternoon. Both options depart from Puerto Banús , just 5 nautical miles from the dolphin-rich waters off Cabopino. Whether you choose a 2-hour sprint or an 8-hour cruise, your charter includes a licensed skipper, drinks, snacks, and insurance. Let’s break down the differences so you can book the perfect slot. Why Cabopino is a dolphin hotspot Cabopino, a small marina east of Marbella, sits near the Río Verde estuary. Nutrient-rich currents attract baitfish, which in turn draw bottlenose and striped dolphins year-round. The area’s moderate depth (15–30 m) and clear water make sightings reliable, especially between May and October when sea temperatures range from 18°C to 26°C. Morning tours benefit from less boat traffic, while afternoon trips enjoy warmer surface layers that often trigger feeding frenzies. Morning tours: calm seas and sharp light Morning charters (typically 9:00–11:00) offer the flattest sea conditions. The Levante wind usually picks up after midday, so early departures mean less rocking – ideal for families with young children or anyone prone to seasickness. The low-angle sun creates excellent photo conditions, illuminating dolphin dorsal fins against deep blue water. Our Astondoa 40 (12.5m, 9 pax) and Azimut 39 (12.5m, 11 pax) are perfect for small groups. At €749 for 2 hours, you get a skipper, fuel, drinks, and snacks included. Morning tours also allow you to combine dolphin watching with a swim stop at Cala del Faro before the crowds arrive. If you’re on an 8-hour charter (€2,299), you can cruise east to Marbella Marina for lunch. Afternoon tours: warmer water and active dolphins Afternoon departures (15:00–17:00) coincide with peak dolphin activity. As the sea warms, dolphins often hunt closer to the surface, making them easier to spot. The golden hour light (18:00–20:00) paints the La Concha mountain in warm hues – a photographer’s dream. Our Mangusta 80 (24m, 12 pax) includes a free jet ski, perfect for adrenaline after dolphin watching. Minimum charter is 4 hours at €4,719. Afternoon tours are also popular for sunset cruises. After spotting dolphins off Cabopino, anchor near Cala Cortés for a swim, then watch the sun dip behind the Golden Mile . The Bandido (11.5m, 8 pax) and Pershing 46 (14m, 10 pax) are excellent choices for a stylish afternoon outing. Comparing morning vs afternoon: key factors Factor Morning (9:00–11:00) Afternoon (15:00–17:00) Sea conditions Flat, minimal wind Light chop possible Light for photos Low-angle, sharp Golden hour, warm Dolphin activity Moderate High (feeding) Boat traffic Low Moderate Best for families Yes (calm) Less ideal Sunset views No Yes Both time slots offer reliable sightings, but the choice comes down to your group’s preferences. For a detailed overview of all our boats, visit our boat rental Marbella page. Which boat should you book? For a 2-hour dolphin-watching trip, the Astondoa 40 or Azimut 39 (both Tier A at €749) are cost-effective. For a full-day adventure, the Azimut 58 Flybridge (17.6m, 12 pax) offers a spacious flybridge for panoramic dolphin spotting. If you want luxury, the Ferretti 94 (29m, 12 pax) provides a sundeck and jacuzzi – perfect for afternoon charters. All bookings include skipper, fuel, drinks, and snacks. See our full fleet for pricing and availability. Practical tips for your trip Book ahead – summer slots fill quickly, especially weekends. Arrive 15 minutes early at Puerto Banús for boarding. Bring sunscreen – even morning sun reflects off the water. Pack a light jacket – morning sea breezes can be cool. Ask your skipper – they know the best dolphin zones and can adjust the route. Frequently asked questions What is the best time of day for Cabopino dolphin watching? Morning tours (9:00–11:00) offer flat seas and good light for photography. Afternoon tours (15:00–17:00) have warmer water and often higher dolphin activity. Both times yield reliable sightings, but mornings are preferred for families with young children. The short transit means more time on the water searching for dolphins. Peak Dolphin Watching Season 2049 The dolphin watching season in Cabopino peaks from June to September. Water temperatures reach 22–26°C, and daylight hours are longest. July and August offer the highest sighting rates, with calm seas and consistent dolphin activity. May and October can also be good, but sightings are less predictable. For the best experience, book a charter between 09:00 and 11:00 or 16:00 and 18:00, when dolphins feed near the surface. TITLE: Cabopino Snorkeling Spots Map: Best Locations by Boat from Marbella
DESCRIPTION: Discover the best Cabopino snorkeling spots map for 2026. Rent a boat from Marbella to reach hidden coves, crystal waters, and marine life. Book now.

Home › Guide › Cabopino Snorkeling Spots Map: Best Locations by Boat from Marbella By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers Cabopino is one of the Costa del Sol's best-kept secrets for snorkelling, with clear waters, rocky reefs, and sandy coves just 15 minutes by boat from Puerto Banús. This guide provides a practical map of the top Cabopino snorkeling spots, boat recommendations from our fleet, and everything you need to plan a day on the water in 2026. Whether you're a beginner or experienced snorkeller, we cover the key locations, depths, and marine life you can expect. Why Cabopino for Snorkeling? Cabopino's coastline is part of a protected natural area, which means minimal development and excellent water quality. The seabed is a mix of sandy patches and Posidonia seagrass meadows, home to sea bream, damselfish, and the occasional octopus. The water clarity averages 10–15m in summer, making it ideal for spotting marine life from the surface. Unlike crowded beaches near Marbella, Cabopino's coves are quieter and offer a more intimate snorkelling experience. Map of Cabopino Snorkeling Spots Our laminated onboard chart marks the following spots, all reachable within a 20-minute cruise from Puerto Banús. The map includes depth markers, buoy locations, and entry points for each cove. Cala del Faro – Sandy bottom, 2–5m deep, calm waters. Best for beginners. Coordinates: 36.4842° N, -4.7553° W. Cala Cortés – Rocky reefs, 3–8m deep. Look for ornate wrasse and cuttlefish. Coordinates: 36.4795° N, -4.7489° W. Punta Calaburras – Deeper spot, 10–15m. Occasional rays and small groupers. Coordinates: 36.4760° N, -4.7400° W. Playa de Cabopino – Sandy beach with gentle slope, 1–4m deep. Good for families. Coordinates: 36.4830° N, -4.7600° W. All spots are sheltered from prevailing westerly winds, but check conditions before anchoring. Our skipper will advise on the best choice based on the day's swell and wind. What to Expect: Marine Life and Conditions The waters around Cabopino are part of a marine protected area, so fishing is restricted, and biodiversity thrives. You'll commonly see salema, damselfish, ornate wrasse, and sea bream. In deeper areas like Punta Calaburras, keep an eye out for stingrays resting on the sandy bottom or cuttlefish camouflaged among rocks. The water temperature ranges from 18°C in May to 26°C in August. We recommend a 3mm wetsuit in spring and autumn for comfort. Visibility is best from June to September, when the water is calm and clear. Avoid days with strong easterly winds (levante), which can stir up sediment. Our skipper will check the forecast before departure. Planning Your Snorkeling Day: Tips and Logistics Departure time: We offer daily slots at 10:00, 13:00, and 16:00. Lunch options: For longer charters, we can arrange a stop at Chiringuito Cabopino or anchor near the beach for a picnic. Booking: Reserve at least 48 hours in advance, especially in July and August. Home › Guide › Best Spots for Dolphin Watching on a Marbella Boat Rental By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers Dolphin watching from a Marbella boat rental is one of the most magical experiences on the Costa del Sol. The Mediterranean waters here are home to several dolphin species, and with the right spot and timing, you can see them leaping alongside your yacht. Whether you're departing from Puerto Banús or exploring the coast, this guide covers the best spots for dolphin watching in Marbella , what to expect, and how to choose the right boat for your trip. Why Marbella Is a Top Destination for Dolphin Watching Marbella's coastline offers a unique mix of deep waters close to shore and rich feeding grounds. The waters off Estepona, Cabopino, and the Strait of Gibraltar are known for high dolphin activity. With water temperatures ranging from 14°C in winter to 28°C in summer, dolphins are present year-round but most visible from April to October. The area's marine biodiversity, including schools of fish and squid, attracts bottlenose, common, and striped dolphins. A boat rental in Marbella gives you access to these prime locations, away from crowded beaches. Best Spots for Dolphin Watching Near Marbella Here are the top locations for dolphin sightings, all reachable within 2-4 hours from Puerto Banús: Estepona Coast (10 NM west) : Known for resident bottlenose dolphins. Calm waters and clear visibility make it a favourite. Cabopino (5 NM east) : A protected area with rich marine life. Common dolphins are frequent here. Strait of Gibraltar (30 NM west) : A migration corridor for striped dolphins and pilot whales. Best for longer trips. Sotogrande (20 NM west) : Deep channels attract feeding dolphins, especially in the morning. Cala del Faro (8 NM east) : A quiet cove where dolphins often play near the rocks. For the best experience, head out early (8-10 AM) or late afternoon (4-6 PM) when dolphins are most active feeding. What Dolphins Can You See? The most common species are bottlenose dolphins (up to 3.5m), common dolphins (up to 2.5m), and striped dolphins (up to 2.4m). Occasionally, you might spot pilot whales or even orcas during their migration in late summer. All sightings are wild and natural—no feeding or touching is allowed to protect the animals. Tips for a Successful Dolphin Watching Trip Book early morning or late afternoon for best sightings. Bring binoculars and a camera with a zoom lens. Wear sunscreen and a hat —the Mediterranean sun is strong. Choose a boat with a flybridge for elevated views (e.g., Mangusta 80 or Azimut 58 Flybridge). Listen to your skipper —they know the local hotspots. Remember, dolphins are wild, so patience is key. Most trips see them within 30-60 minutes of reaching the right area. In this guide, we break down the marbella dolphin watching best time of day, so you can maximise your chances of a memorable encounter. Why Morning Trips Offer the Best Sighting Odds Early morning, between 8:00 and 10:00, is widely considered the prime window. The sea is usually calm after the overnight lull, with fewer boats on the water. Dolphins are often actively hunting for breakfast, making them more visible at the surface. Our skippers at Puerto Banús recommend a morning departure for the highest probability of spotting pods. The Sunset Alternative: Atmosphere Over Certainty Late afternoon, from 17:00 to 19:00, offers a different kind of magic. While dolphin activity can be slightly less predictable, the golden light over the Sierra Blanca and La Concha makes for stunning photography. A sunset charter on the Astondoa 40 or Azimut 39 combines dolphin watching with a coastal cruise past the Golden Mile – a popular choice for couples. Seasonal Patterns: When to Go for Peak Sightings Dolphins are present off Marbella year-round, but the best months are May to October. June and September typically offer the most consistent sightings, with water temperatures between 20°C and 24°C. In summer, early morning trips are cooler and more comfortable, while autumn afternoons can be equally productive. Our Fleet and Departure Times All our boats depart from Puerto Banús. Midday trips are possible but sightings can be less predictable. Are dolphins more active in the morning or at sunset? Both are good. Mornings typically have calmer water and less boat traffic, making it easier to spot dolphins. Sunsets offer warmer light and a more atmospheric experience. We recommend morning for the highest sighting probability. Which months are best for dolphin watching in Marbella? May through October is prime season. June and September tend to have the most consistent sightings, with water temperatures around 20–24°C. Dolphins are present year-round, but winter seas can be rougher, reducing sighting chances. What types of dolphins can I see in Marbella? The most common are bottlenose dolphins and striped dolphins. Occasionally, you might spot common dolphins or even pilot whales further offshore. Sightings are not guaranteed, but our skippers know the best areas. How long is a typical dolphin watching trip from Puerto Banús? Our Tier A boats (Astondoa 40 and Azimut 39) offer 2-hour and 8-hour charters. A 2-hour trip is ideal for a quick dolphin encounter; the 8-hour option lets you explore further along the coast and increases your chances of multiple sightings. TITLE: Dolphin Watching Season in Marbella: Month by Month

Home › Guide › Dolphin Watching Season in Marbella: Month by Month By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers Marbella's coastline, with the Sierra Blanca mountains as a backdrop and the glittering waters of the Alboran Sea, is one of Europe's best spots for dolphin watching. The marbella dolphin watching season runs from May to September, but with the right boat and a bit of luck, you can spot these marine mammals almost any month of the year. In this guide, we break down the season month by month, so you know exactly when to book your charter from Puerto Banús. Why Marbella is a Dolphin Hotspot The waters off Marbella sit on the edge of the Alboran Sea, a nutrient-rich area where the Atlantic and Mediterranean meet. This creates a feeding ground for dolphins, whales, and sea turtles. The continental shelf drops steeply just a few nautical miles offshore, bringing deep-water species close to the coast. On a typical 2-hour trip from Puerto Banús, you can reach the feeding grounds off Cabopino or Cala del Faro in under 30 minutes. Month-by-Month Dolphin Activity Month Sea Temp Dolphin Activity Best Boat Choice January–February 15–17°C Low; rough seas Mangusta 80 (24m) March–April 17–19°C Moderate; improving Azimut 58 Flybridge May 20–22°C High; season begins Astondoa 40 or Azimut 39 June–August 24–28°C Peak; daily sightings Any boat; early morning best September 23–25°C High; still warm Fairline Targa 12m October 20–22°C Moderate; good conditions Pershing 46 November–December 16–18°C Low; choppier seas Canados 86 (26m) for stability Spring (March–May): The Season Builds March still has cool water (17°C), but by May the sea warms to 20–22°C and the dolphin pods become more active. Sea temperatures reach 25–30°C, and the Mediterranean is at its calmest. Dolphin sightings are almost daily, especially in the early morning. Book a 4-hour charter on the Mangusta 80 (€4,719) — it comes with a free jet ski, so you can chase the pods and then enjoy a swim in a secluded cove like Cala Cortés. The 24m length gives you stability and speed to cover more ground. Autumn (September–October): Still Excellent September is a hidden gem. The water is still 23–25°C, and the summer crowds have thinned. Dolphins remain active as they feed before the cooler months. October is also good, though you'll want to check the wind forecast. The Fairline Targa 12m (12m, 10 pax) is a nimble choice for a 2-hour excursion, and with the season guide in mind, you'll know exactly when to go. Winter (November–February): The Challenging Months Winter brings rougher seas and cooler temperatures (15–18°C). Dolphins are still present, but sighting probability drops. If you're determined, choose a larger vessel like the Canados 86 (26m) or the Ferretti 94 (29m) for maximum stability. Skippers will often head toward the deeper waters near the Strait of Gibraltar, where pods gather. Expect a bumpier ride, but the reward is a rare winter encounter. How to Maximise Your Dolphin Sighting Chances Go early: 9:00–11:00 is prime time. Choose a calm day: wind under 15 knots. Book a 4-hour charter: more time = more chances. Responsible Dolphin Watching We follow the Spanish marine mammal protection guidelines: no chasing, no sudden turns, and maintaining a safe distance of at least 60 metres. Our skippers are trained to observe without disturbing. This ensures the dolphins return year after year, keeping Marbella a premier destination for boat charters . Frequently asked questions What is the best month for dolphin watching in Marbella? June, July, and August are the best months. TITLE: Estepona vs Cabopino: Best Snorkeling Spots by Boat from Marbella
DESCRIPTION: Estepona vs Cabopino snorkeling by boat from Marbella: compare clarity, marine life, and distances. Book your charter and dive in today.

Home › Guide › Estepona vs Cabopino: Best Snorkeling Spots by Boat from Marbella By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers When you're planning a boat trip from Marbella, the choice between Estepona and Cabopino for snorkeling can be a tough one. Both offer beautiful Mediterranean waters, but they differ in character, marine life, and distance from Puerto Banús. This guide breaks down the key differences—so you can pick the spot that suits your group, your budget, and your snorkeling experience. Estepona vs Cabopino: The Quick Answer If you want crystal-clear water and rocky coves teeming with fish, choose Estepona. It's about 15 nautical miles (40 minutes) from Puerto Banús, and the underwater scenery is more dramatic—rocky reefs, seagrass beds, and a greater variety of marine species. Cabopino, on the other hand, is closer (7 NM, ~20 minutes) and offers calm, shallow sandy areas, perfect for beginners or families with kids. The water is slightly less clear, but the setting—dunes and pine forest—is uniquely charming. Distance and Travel Time from Marbella Distance is a major factor. Cabopino is a quick hop from Puerto Banús, making it ideal for a 2- or 3-hour charter. Estepona requires a bit more commitment—a 40-minute cruise each way—so it's better suited to half-day (4-5 hours) or full-day trips. On a Tier A boat like the Astondoa 40 (€749/2h) or Azimut 39, you can comfortably reach either destination and still have plenty of time in the water. Spot Distance from Puerto Banús Travel Time Best for Cabopino 7 NM ~20 min Short trips, beginners, families Estepona 15 NM ~40 min Clear water, experienced snorkelers Water Clarity and Visibility Estepona's water is generally clearer because the coastline is rockier and there's less sand suspended in the water. Visibility often exceeds 10 metres in summer. Cabopino, being close to the dunes, has more sediment, so visibility is typically 5-8 metres. If you're keen on underwater photography or spotting shy fish, Estepona is the winner. Marine Life: What You'll See Around Estepona's rocky reefs, you'll encounter damselfish, wrasses, sea bream, and occasionally octopus and moray eels. The seagrass meadows near Cabopino are home to juvenile fish, pipefish, and sometimes seahorses (if you're lucky). Both spots are protected from heavy boat traffic, so the fish are less skittish. For sheer diversity, Estepona takes the lead. Best Spots for Snorkeling Near Estepona, head to the coves around Punta de la Doncella or the rocky areas just off the old town—your skipper will know the exact spots. For Cabopino, the sandy shallows near the dunes are safe and easy. You can also anchor just off the beach and swim in—there's a protected marine area that keeps the water calm. Boat Options and Pricing For a snorkeling trip, the Astondoa 40 or Azimut 39 are perfect—spacious, comfortable, and priced at €749 for 2 hours or €2,299 for 8 hours (skipper, drinks, snacks, VAT included). If you want a bit more luxury and a complimentary jet ski, the Mangusta 80 (from €4,719 for 4 hours) offers a flybridge and plenty of deck space. For larger groups, consider the Azimut 58 Flybridge or the Ferretti 94 —pricing on request via WhatsApp. Practical Tips for Your Snorkeling Trip Book a morning departure—the sea is calmest and visibility is best. Bring a thin wetsuit or rash vest; water temps range from 18°C in June to 24°C in August. Use reef-safe sunscreen to protect the marine environment. Ask your skipper to anchor away from seagrass beds to avoid damaging them. If you're new to snorkeling, Cabopino's calm waters are safer for practice. Our Verdict: Which Should You Choose? Choose Estepona if you're an experienced snorkeler or want the clearest water and most vibrant marine life. Choose Cabopino if you have limited time, are travelling with kids, or prefer a relaxed, shallow-water experience. Both are easily accessible from Marbella, and with our boat rental options , you can tailor the trip to your exact needs. For a full-day adventure, you could even visit both—start at Cabopino for a warm-up, then head to Estepona for the main event. Frequently asked questions Which is better for snorkeling: Estepona or Cabopino? Estepona generally wins for snorkeling because the water is clearer and the coastline is rockier, which attracts more fish and marine life. Cabopino has sandy bottoms and seagrass meadows, which are nice but less visually dramatic. If you want vibrant underwater scenery, head to Estepona; if you prefer calm, shallow waters for beginners, Cabopino is a good choice. How long does it take to reach Estepona by boat from Marbella? From Puerto Banús, it takes about 40 minutes at cruising speed to cover the 15 nautical miles to Estepona. On a Tier A boat like the Astondoa 40, you can expect a smooth ride. This makes Estepona a perfect destination for a half-day or full-day charter, giving you plenty of time to snorkel, swim, and enjoy the coast. How far is Cabopino from Marbella by boat? Cabopino is only about 7 nautical miles from Puerto Banús, roughly 20 minutes at a relaxed pace. It's the closest good snorkeling spot to Marbella, making it ideal for shorter trips or if you have limited time. You can easily combine a morning snorkel at Cabopino with lunch at a chiringuito on the beach. What marine life can I see snorkeling near Estepona? Around Estepona's rocky coves and reefs, you can spot damselfish, wrasses, sea bream, and occasionally octopus and moray eels. The underwater rock formations provide shelter for many species. Snorkeling near the Punta de la Doncella or the protected area of the Estepona coast offers the best chances of seeing a variety of fish. Is Cabopino good for beginner snorkelers? Yes, Cabopino is excellent for beginners. The water is shallow and calm, with sandy bottoms and patches of seagrass. The gentle conditions make it easy to practice snorkeling, and the visibility is usually good in summer. There are also dunes and a pine forest behind the beach, adding a scenic backdrop to your trip. What is the best time of year for snorkeling in Marbella? The best months are June through September, when sea temperatures range from 18°C in June to 24°C in August. Visibility is typically highest in late summer after the spring plankton blooms settle. If you're booking a boat charter, aim for morning departures when the sea is calmest and the sun is high enough to light the seabed. Can I rent a boat for snorkeling without a skipper? No, all our charters include a licensed skipper. This is for your safety and convenience—the skipper knows the best snorkeling spots and handles navigation, anchoring, and safety. You can relax and focus on the underwater world. The skipper can also advise on current conditions and where the fish are biting. What is included in the boat rental price? Every charter includes a licensed skipper, fuel, water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava, light snacks, insurance, safety equipment, and Spanish IVA (21%). So the price you see is the price you pay—no hidden extras. For Tier A boats (Astondoa 40, Azimut 39), the cost is €749 for 2 hours or €2,299 for 8 hours. ★★★★★ 4.9 · verified reviews From €749 2h skippered charter Direct quotes from our own Puerto Banús fleet — same boats, same skippers, no third-party hand-off. 💬 Message on WhatsApp Avg reply < 5 min · No deposit until you confirm 📅 Book now Browse the full fleet → Compare live prices on BoatHire24 → All charters include Licensed skipper & fuel Drinks: water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava Light snacks Insurance & safety equipment Snorkel gear & inflatables VAT 21% — no surprises

Other boats like the Ferretti 94 or Canados 86 are priced on request via WhatsApp – expect €8,000–€15,000 for a full day. What's included in the boat rental price? Every charter includes a licensed skipper, fuel, water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava, light snacks, insurance, safety equipment, and Spanish IVA (21%). No hidden costs – you just show up at Puerto Banús and step aboard. For longer charters, you can request a stop at a chiringuito for lunch (lunch not included). How far in advance should I book for the F1 weekend? F1 weekends in Marbella are extremely busy. We recommend booking at least 2–3 months in advance, especially for the larger yachts (Mangusta 80, Ferretti 94). For the Tier A boats, 4–6 weeks ahead is usually enough, but last-minute availability is rare. Contact us via WhatsApp to secure your preferred vessel. Can we anchor near the beach to watch the race? Yes, anchoring is allowed in certain areas off the coast of Marbella, but you must respect the nautical safety zones and local regulations. Your skipper will know the best spots, such as off Cabopino or near the Marbella Marina, where you can set anchor and enjoy the race on a portable screen. Remember, the skipper has the final say for safety. What if the weather is bad on race day? If the sea conditions are unsafe, we will reschedule your charter or offer a full refund. In Marbella, May–June typically has calm seas with 20–25°C temperatures, so the risk is low. However, we always monitor the forecast and will contact you 24 hours in advance if any changes are needed. Ready to make your F1 weekend unforgettable? Explore our boats and book your spot today. Instead of fighting the crowds on land, why not watch the action from the deck of a private boat? With departures from Puerto Banús, you can combine the thrill of the race with the luxury of the Costa del Sol. Here's everything you need to know about renting a boat for the F1 weekend – from pricing to the best boats and itineraries. Why choose a boat for the F1 weekend? Marbella transforms during the F1 Grand Prix weekend. Hotels fill up, beach clubs get crowded, and traffic on the Golden Mile can be a nightmare. A boat gives you a private, air-conditioned escape – you can anchor in a quiet cove, enjoy the sea breeze, and still be part of the action. Plus, you get front-row seats to the sunset over La Concha, with a glass of cava in hand. All our boats depart from Puerto Banús, just 10 minutes from Marbella Marina. You can choose from a range of vessels, from sporty speedboats to 29-metre superyachts. Whether you're a couple or a group of 12, there's a boat that fits your needs. Sample itinerary for race day Here's how to make the most of your F1 boat day: 11:00 – Depart Puerto Banús, cruise along the coast towards Cabopino. 12:00 – Anchor for a swim and enjoy the included snacks and drinks. 14:00 – Set up a screen on deck to watch the F1 race live (you can bring your own tablet or use your phone). 16:30 – After the race, cruise back to Puerto Banús for the post-race celebrations. 19:00 – Dock at Marbella Marina and continue the evening at a beach club.
